     - **Concurrent forgets** (review): every ResponseCache open used to delete all marks, including those of a forget still running in another process or another MemorySystem, which then had to pay for all its calls again, and a false "process exited midway" warning was logged. Now every hold takes a lock: an exclusive transaction on a lock-only SQLite file under `<cache>.holds/<token>` (cross-platform, and visible to other connections in the same process), and the marks record their owner (a new owner column, migrated on open). An open deletes only marks whose lock is free (and legacy marks without an owner) with their lock files; settle reconciles only its own marks and releases the lock after the commit; close() releases the locks and leaves the marks for the next open.
     - **Measured** (a copy of data/cache, synthetic replies for misses; forgetting the middle session of 7 LoCoMo pds spaces and 12 LongMemEval default spaces): reconciliation and entity requests sent again fell from 580 to 28 (LoCoMo 231 → 23, LongMemEval 349 → 5, with 10 of the 12 LongMemEval spaces sending none). Checked space by space: every entry left in the consolidate scope has a key that the replay itself computed, and none of the 132 fact statements produced only by the forgotten session appears in a kept entry. The regrowth loop still rebuilds locally once per round (only a rebuild shows whether a fact grew back), and each round re-requests only the reconciliation and entity calls whose input changed.
     - **Changed defaults:** the Consolidator uses more memory: the mirrors of 4 spaces (about 40 MB per 10k facts at 1024 dimensions) and up to 100,000 session digests; `enqueue` commits the jobs of one call at once; after a forget, the derived memory of unaffected history keeps its original LLM decisions instead of being asked again, so `ForgetReport.llm_cache_purged` is lower; llm.sqlite gains a table (created on open, no version bump) and a `.holds/` directory next to it; opening the cache sweeps marks left by a forget that died and logs a warning.
     - **Evaluation:** evaluations never forget. Consolidation of all 414 published CueMem evaluation spaces (LoCoMo pds 7, LoCoMo qwen 7, and 100 each of LongMemEval with the default and qwen presets × raw and rewritten session ids) was replayed read-only: a read-only copy of llm.sqlite as the LLM cache (a miss raises), and embed.sqlite opened with `mode=ro` and no inner embedder, recording every cache key in order and every text sent for embedding. Base, the R3 commit and the final commit agree: 32,275 cache keys equal in order; 94,704 facts, 62,849 entities, and the episodes, profiles, units and job statistics equal field by field; every space's consolidation fingerprint matches the recorded one. Multi-run replays, where the mirror is actually reused (22 spaces: 7 LoCoMo bge, 7 LoCoMo qwen, 8 LongMemEval default; three modes: one session consolidated per run; the same, then a middle session forgotten and consolidated again; SDK-style enqueue of all sessions before each run), 66 replays in all: 5,750 cache keys equal in order (44 of them outside data/cache, answered by the same synthetic reply on both sides), the 18 embedding-miss requests equal in order and content digest, and 16,911 facts and 5,401 entities equal field by field.
  3. **Read-path scans and start-up** (R5, R7; revises D-030, D-036, D-040, D-056, D-060):
     - **Fewer full scans** (R5): when a space has no units of the configured caption kind (the new `MemoryDB.has_units`, an indexed LIMIT 1), L1 does not search image captions and records the route as empty, and the sparse key likewise; summary search whose fusion weight is 0 is skipped (`L1Retriever.candidates(probe=False)`; the probe passes probe=True, so the p_summary_* features are unchanged). `candidates()` records a `RecallKey` (the question text, the dense vector's dtype and bytes, the pool, the sparse route actually used, the unit kinds and the exclude set) and accepts `reuse=`: when the key matches bitwise and the reused result covers the needed kinds, the probe's text routes (summary excepted) are reused and re-scored with the current QueryInfo and configuration; text-to-image search is still embedded and searched as before. `MemorySystem.retrieve` passes the probe's candidates to `L2Retriever.search(probe=)`; `Recall` carries `candidates`; `retrieve(mode='l1', candidates=...)` packs them directly; `recall_listing` computes the question time once and passes the probe's candidates back when it raises L0 to L1. Local Qdrant scans per query: L1 4 → 2, the probe 4 → 3, auto → L2 no longer searches the original question again, and recall raised from L0 in MCP, Agents and LangGraph 8 → 3; raising no longer embeds the question a second time (one embedding request fewer without the embedding cache, one cache lookup fewer with it). Replayed on 19 copied evaluation spaces (7 LoCoMo qwen, 12 LongMemEval default) × 1,501 questions in evaluation order × 6 flows (B6, L1 = B4-nobm25, B4 with BM25, B6 forced to L2, B5-noplan, and recall_listing raising L0 to L1; query vectors read from data/cache only; deterministic planner and checker stand-ins that produce sub-queries and follow-ups): all 9,006 results are bitwise identical to before (ids, ranks, score reprs, decisions and their scores, the inputs of L2's plan and check), and vector searches fell from 73,376 to 35,021.
     - **A limit found along the way:** qdrant-client's local mode renormalises the whole collection in place on every search (`qdrant_client/local/distances.py`), so scores depend on how many searches the process has made before. Collections loaded from disk (float64) stop changing after 4–6 searches; float32 collections created in the process can keep oscillating, and exactly tied results can reorder. This was already so before the change: on a synthetic hash-vector space with many ties, 34 of 288 results reorder tied items across the change, while the old code alone reorders 65 of 288 after one extra warm-up search. Parts 1 and 2 above are therefore not bitwise identical by construction; they were checked bitwise on real spaces.
     - **Faster start-up** (R7): the package `__init__` files of embed, store, llm, read, write and agent import lazily (a module-level `__getattr__` plus TYPE_CHECKING imports), and the old import paths still work; shared Literal aliases (AskMode, RecallMode, Router, Scenario, PeakPolicy) move to the new `cuemem/core/types.py`, re-exported from their old modules; the CLI imports the memory system, evaluations, the service and the agent inside its commands, sdk.py imports MemorySystem inside `Memory()`, and matplotlib and scipy are imported only when plotting or building the graph. Median of 7 runs on the development machine: `cuemem --help` 3.8 → 0.42 s, `import cuemem.sdk` 2.7 → 0.30 s, `import cuemem.memory` 2.6 → 2.1 s, `cuemem mcp` from start to exit 4.35 → 3.37 s (it really needs qdrant_client, openai and the mcp SDK; with the local extra installed, qdrant_client imports fastembed itself).
     - **Lighter dependencies** (R7): new extras `ui` (Gradio) and `eval` (matplotlib and scikit-learn, for evaluation charts and the controller and gate training scripts; controller inference does not use scikit-learn). The core install keeps the SDK, the CLI, the HTTP API, the proxy and MCP, and drops from 87 to 54 distributions (Linux, Python 3.12). The dev group adds `cuemem[ui,eval]`, so `uv sync` in the repository installs the same set as before; after `uv lock`, only cuemem's own metadata changed (145 packages, no version changes). Without the ui extra, `cuemem serve` serves the HTTP API and the `/v1` proxy and prints how to install the UI (`uv sync --extra ui` or `pip install "cuemem[ui]"`), and `create_app` logs the same warning; without matplotlib, `cuemem eval run` (except `--build-only`) and `eval report` say which extra to install and exit 1 at the start, while split, probe, audit and data download work. Docker's default `EXTRAS` becomes `--extra ui --extra local`; `--build-arg EXTRAS=` builds an image with only the API and the proxy. CI: the test job installs the ui, eval and integrations extras; the package job asserts that the core install has no gradio, matplotlib or scikit-learn, runs `mcp --help` and `proxy --help`, checks the eval hint and runs `cuemem serve` without `--no-ui` (health check, and the ui hint in the log); the docker job checks that gradio is in the image. The package job was run locally: the wheel installed with the locked core requirements in a fresh environment outside the repository, and every step passed.
     - **Changed defaults:** `pip install cuemem` no longer installs gradio, matplotlib or scikit-learn (nor pandas, huggingface-hub and so on); `cuemem serve` without the ui extra serves only the API and the proxy (gradio was a core dependency until now, so that path never ran); the Docker image includes the ui extra by default; `recall_listing` uses one question time for both retrievals when at=None (before, each took its own `datetime.now()`). New `cuemem.config.extra_remedy()` and `cuemem.core.types`.
     - **Evaluation:** retrieval results, probe features, controller decisions and requests are unchanged (the replays above), and the frozen-request guards pass. The published N11 latency table was not re-run; a re-run should be faster in local mode.
  4. **Evaluation: the command, the datasets and multiple comparisons** (R8, R9; revises D-015, D-016, D-057, and the significance claims in D-030, D-033, D-035, D-037, D-041, D-055 and D-065):
     - **One question-selection rule:** a new `datasets.protocol_samples(evalset, types=None)`: with types, keep only those types; otherwise LoCoMo drops its adversarial questions and other datasets keep everything. `eval run`, `eval probe`, `n7_mixed.dev_samples` and `n9_mixed.test_samples` all call it (it used to be copied four times). Old and new rules select identical questions on every LoCoMo and LongMemEval split (all, dev3 497 → 385, test7 1489 → 1155, test200r, dev100, pilot10, test100r, test400) under 5 spellings of `--types`; 54 of the 57 run.json files in docs/results and runs/eval have matching sample counts, and the other 3 are N12 leak sanity runs that picked question ids by hand without the CLI; the same-topic hard negatives of the mixed streams correspond exactly to the selected questions (N7 485, N9 1,555). `cuemem eval run` has offline tests through the real runner (method selection, `--limit` and `--types`, resume, LoCoMo's adversarial questions, the budget stop, error exit codes).
     - **Datasets pinned to upstream commits:** `DatasetSpec` gains a sha256 field and extra files become (filename, URL, sha256); every URL is pinned to a 40-hex commit: LongMemEval `xiaowu0162/longmemeval-cleaned@98d7416`, LoCoMo `snap-research/locomo@3eb6f2c`, Dolly `databricks/databricks-dolly-15k@bdd27f4`, MemLens `xiyuRenBill/MEMLENS@afa101a`, and WildChat still at `7d6490e`. Downloads are hashed while streaming, and the .part file is renamed only when the hash matches; a mismatch raises the new `DatasetChecksumError`, and the .part file is removed on any error; a tarball is checked before it is extracted. `cuemem data download` prints "错误：下载的 … 与固定的版本不同…" and exits 1 on a mismatch. Files already on disk are skipped as before and not re-hashed. The LongMemEval and LoCoMo hashes were checked against the local copies in data/datasets, Dolly by reconstructing the committed dolly_lines*.json, and MemLens and WildChat come from the upstream LFS metadata at the pinned commit (there is no local copy).
     - **Holm correction:** `routing.holm` (Holm–Bonferroni; monotone, capped at 1, key order kept) and `routing.holm_family` (which recomputes the exact sign-test p from wins and losses, because published p values are rounded and tiny ones are written as 0). A new `scripts/multiplicity.py` defines the families of every published result, listed in its docstring: N9's per-dataset overall comparisons, each comparison by question type, the controller and the reader swap; N12's "L1 and L2 vs the external systems" (10 tests), "CueMem and the references" and the sensitivity run; the leak ablation per method, pooled, and vs Mem0 per arm; N11's Mem0, second judge, controller v2 and photo gate; N7's controller and H3. A result with more than one family also gets a "全文" (whole document) family as the most conservative bound; mirrored pairs count once, and +0 / −0 is not a test. The results go to 10 `*-holm.json` files next to the results (`uv run python scripts/multiplicity.py` recomputes them from the published results without runs/); the 9 scripts that produce those results call it after writing their JSON, so a re-run rewrites the same files and prints, per family, how many tests are nominally significant, how many survive and which are lost; `paired_compare.py` uses `routing.sign_test` and gains a "Holm 校正后" column. Accuracies, wins and losses and raw p values are untouched.
     - **Claims that are no longer significant** (α = 0.05 within a family; raw p → adjusted p in parentheses):
       - **N12 external baselines:** under pds, L2 vs Mem0 +17 / −5 (0.017 → 0.068), so "L2 is significantly ahead of all five external systems" fails for Mem0 and still holds for LangMem (0.011), Cognee (0.011), Graphiti and A-MEM (< 0.001); L2 vs the controller +7 / −0 under pds (0.016 → 0.078). Under pq, L2 vs Mem0 (0.014 → 0.041) and L1 vs Cognee (0.017 → 0.041) survive the 10-test family but not the 20-test whole-document family (0.12, 0.13), and neither does L1 vs A-MEM with k = 40 under pds (0.024 → 0.19).
       - **N9 LoCoMo:** L2 vs L1 overall still holds (p < 0.001), and by type multi-hop (0.0013) and single-hop (0.010) survive, but open-domain +8 / −1 and temporal +15 / −5 (both about 0.04 → 0.078) do not, so "significant on each of the four types" is wrong; L2 2.5 points above full context (0.021 → 0.107) and L1 2.3 points below (0.038 → 0.151); in the controller family, B6 vs the rule-based B6r +34 / −16 (0.015 → 0.061); with qwen3.8-flash as reader, B3 vs L2 +15 / −5 (0.041 → 0.116).
       - **N9 LongMemEval and MemLens:** B6a vs L2 +5 / −18 (0.011 → 0.085); with the smallest reader, B6 vs L2 0 / −7 (0.016 → 0.078); in the controller family, B6 vs always-L2 (0.035 → 0.38). On MemLens 32K, L2 vs full context (0.029 → 0.087); B6 vs always-L2 at 32K (0.0074 → 0.052) and 128K (0.023 → 0.067).
       - **N11:** L1 vs Mem0 on LongMemEval +13 / −4 (0.049 → 0.147); under the second judge, B6a vs L2 on LongMemEval (0.017 → 0.068) and B6 vs L1 on LoCoMo (0.029 → 0.117); 7 single-dataset tests of controller v2. **Leak ablation:** after the fix, L1 vs Mem0 +15 / −4 (0.019 → 0.058), and L2 vs Mem0 in the label arm and the pq raw arm lands just above 0.05. **N7:** most single-dataset controller claims at p 0.008–0.04; in H3, the three "vs B3" tests on LongMemEval after switching to Qwen (0.035 → 0.105), and always-L1 and B6r before vs after the reader swap on LoCoMo (0.055, 0.059).
       - **Claims that appear only in a README** and have no JSON were recomputed in this docs pass from the wins and losses in the README (`routing.sign_test` and `routing.holm`): of N6's 4 development-set tests, re-perception +8 / −1 (0.039 → 0.117), captions +13 / −3 (0.021 → 0.085) and L2 vs L1 +13 / −4 (0.049 → 0.117) do not survive; of N5's 3 development-set tests against the current L1, B6r +8 / −1 (0.039 → 0.117) does not; of N5's 9 LoCoMo tests against L2, L2 vs B4 +27 / −11 (0.014 → 0.11) and vs B6r +21 / −9 (0.043 → 0.30) do not. Still holding: N5's LoCoMo B5-ppr vs the current L1 +22 / −7 (0.008 → 0.024), N6's nominally significant test-set comparisons at both lengths, and the three 32K → 128K pairs (when corrected as those three; counting B4-mm and ORACLE, also compared across lengths, they do not). N9's pooled B6 vs always-L1 over three datasets, +60 / −37 (p = 0.025), has no stored pooled per-question results; in a family of 2 with the pooled comparison against always-L2 it stays at 0.025.
       - The documents (READMEs, the technical report and the per-node result records) keep these numbers, label the claims as nominal and add the adjusted p, or say that the claim does not survive correction; the ADRs get a note after the original entry.
     - **Remaining limits:** N12's tests are conditional on one build per system, and N11's repeated runs exclude write-path randomness; a cluster bootstrap by conversation and repeated builds were not done.
     - **Changed defaults:** `cuemem data download` fetches from the pinned commits and exits 1 on a hash mismatch; new `cuemem.evals.datasets.protocol_samples`, `cuemem.errors.DatasetChecksumError`, `routing.holm` and `holm_family`; no new switches.
     - **Evaluation:** selected questions, requests and cache keys are unchanged; the controller files and the published accuracies, wins and losses and raw p values are unchanged.
  5. **Mem0 compatibility and the remote SDK** (R10, R11; revises D-019, D-061, D-063):
     - **Metadata and expiry:** `add(metadata=, expiration_date=)`. The metadata must be a JSON object with string keys; it is stored with the expiration_date (a date, a datetime or an ISO string; a bare date lasts to the end of that day) in a new raw-layer table, `turn_meta`, only for turns actually inserted and in the same transaction as the turns, and it is kept out of extraction prompts and the session content hash, so requests and cache keys are unchanged. Facts, excerpts and images carry `metadata` (the merge of the source turns' metadata, the later key winning) and `expiration_date` (the latest among the sources; none if any source has none). SQLite's SCHEMA_VERSION goes from 5 to 6, and an older database gets the table with `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` on open (checked against a database and an export written by the old code). The rows are deleted with forgotten turns, by delete_space and by reset.
     - **Filters:** keys other than user_id, agent_id and run_id are metadata conditions with Mem0's operators: eq, ne, gt, gte, lt, lte, in, nin, contains, icontains, `*`, and AND, OR and NOT (NOT = none match); user_id, agent_id and run_id may also appear inside conditions (for example `{"run_id": {"in": [...]}}`); a bad operator raises ValueError. Before, any other key raised `Unsupported filters`. Filtering happens after retrieval, so results can be fewer than the limit.
     - **Expiry:** expired memories are excluded from search, get_all and the profile (search's profile field and `profile()`); get and history still return them, and only delete removes them. Expiry applies when the SDK reads, not by rewriting valid_to in consolidation, so `answer()`, the proxy, MCP, the HTTP `/api` and the integrations still see expired memories until they are deleted.
     - **update():** `update(memory_id, data, metadata=None)` realises the alternative D-061 recommended: it logs the user turn "Correction: <data>" in a new `edit-<time>` session ordered after the target, records `edit_of = memory_id` in the raw layer and consolidates synchronously. `Reconciler.reconcile` gains `corrections=` (turn id → the fact id it corrects, read from turn_meta by the consolidator): among the facts extracted from the correction it picks one (same predicate first, then the closest statement vector, then extraction order), which supersedes the target as corrected without an LLM call; the other facts reconcile as usual. Relying on the reconciliation LLM alone is not enough: registered multi-valued predicates (likes, visited) are always added without an LLM call, so the old memory would stay current. Without edit_of the logic is inert: one extra turn_meta SELECT per consolidated session, no request change, and the frozen digests pass. The new fact inherits the target's metadata (overridable) and expiry; only the current version can be updated (ValueError), and an unknown id raises KeyError; the correction survives rebuild and replay, and forgetting it restores the original. If nothing is extracted, the target is unchanged and the correction is forgotten from the log as well, with `turn_ids` empty (review: before, it stayed in the log, came back from search next to the unchanged fact, went into exports, and its edit_of could apply on a later re-extraction).
     - **reset():** ends the open runs and calls `delete_space` on every space in the data directory (including spaces written by the HTTP service, the proxy and the demo).
     - **search never below L1:** `search(min_level="l1")` is the default: in mode="auto", when the controller picks L0, it retrieves again at L1 (with the probe's retrieval) with reasons `["raised from L0: the caller asked to recall"]`, the same as the recall of MCP and the integrations (`at_least_l1=True`); `min_level="l0"` keeps the controller's decision; an explicit mode is obeyed; `answer()` is unchanged. The "no memory needed" line of `examples/quickstart.py` now passes `min_level="l0"`.
     - **Export format 2:** turns may carry metadata, expires_at and edit_of (omitted when empty); version-1 files still import, and an older CueMem rejects version 2 as a newer format; a bad metadata type fails the whole import before anything is written. The derived-memory snapshot in an export carries no metadata; the per-turn raw metadata is the source of truth. edit_of stores a fact id, which derives from the space and the extraction, so after importing into another space name or switching the extraction model the correction is reconciled as an ordinary "Correction: …" message.
     - **A Mem0-shaped HTTP API** (R11): `cuemem serve` offers, under `/api/v1`, one route per SDK method (`api/memories.py`, wrapping `Memory.from_system` and sharing the memory system and authentication with the UI and the proxy): `POST /memories` (add), `POST /memories/search`, `GET /memories` (get_all; filters as a JSON string), `GET` / `PUT` / `DELETE /memories/{id}`, `GET /memories/{id}/history`, `DELETE /memories` (delete_all), `POST /reset`, `POST /answer`, `GET /profile`, `POST /runs` and `POST /runs/end`, `POST /consolidate`, `POST /wait`, `GET /export`, `POST /import` and `GET /spaces`. Every route is scoped by user_id and agent_id, never by raw space ids. Errors are English, `{"error": {"code", "type", "message"}}`, where type is the exception class the SDK raised; 401, 422 and unknown routes under `/api/v1` get the same shape, and every other path keeps FastAPI's `{"detail"}`. Images are accepted only as data URLs: local paths and file:// get 400 (as in the proxy), because the server does not read its own disk.
     - **MemoryClient:** `from cuemem import MemoryClient` (and `AsyncMemoryClient`, in `cuemem/client.py`). Every public method of Memory except from_config and from_system exists with an identical signature (parameter names, kinds, defaults, annotations and return annotation, guarded by a test). The client raises the same exceptions as Memory (KeyError, ValueError, TypeError and the cuemem.errors types), and other HTTP failures raise the new `cuemem.errors.MemoryAPIError` (with status and code). It reads local images into data URLs, validates metadata as the SDK does (JSON would silently turn non-string keys into strings), falls back to `CUEMEM_API_TOKEN` when no api_key is given, ends the runs it opened on `close()`, and imports only httpx2, not the memory stack. The review closed two differences from the local SDK: embedder errors carry `upstream_status` in the response, from which MemoryClient rebuilds an EmbeddingAPIError with status and retry_after; exporting an unknown space gives 404 (KeyError), and importing a file without space_id when neither user_id nor agent_id is given raises ValueError both locally and remotely (400), so `/api` import now returns 409 instead of 404.
     - **Changed defaults:** search defaults to at least L1 (auto mode no longer returns an empty L0 result); every memory item gains the `metadata` and `expiration_date` keys; other filter keys no longer raise; export format 2; SQLite schema 6; `/api/v1` is always mounted and uses the same token as `/api`. New `Memory.update`, `Memory.reset`, `cuemem.MemoryClient`, `cuemem.AsyncMemoryClient` and `cuemem.errors.MemoryAPIError`. No new CLI flags, environment variables or extras.
     - **Evaluation:** evaluations never use the SDK or write turn_meta; requests and cache keys are unchanged.
  6. **Multi-tenancy** (R12; revises D-056):
     - **Scoped keys:** `CUEMEM_API_TOKEN` stays the admin token. New: `CUEMEM_API_KEYS_FILE` (the path of a YAML file, relative paths resolved against the current directory) or `CUEMEM_API_KEYS` (the same content as inline YAML or JSON); only one of the two may be set. The format is `{keys: [{name, sha256 | token, scopes: [{user?, agent?}]}]}`: `{user: X}` is all of X's memory (without an agent and under any agent), `{user: U, agent: A}` only U under A, and `{agent: A}` only A's own memory (agent/A); a value is an ID or a list, `*` matches any ID, and a trailing `*` is a prefix (`acme:*`); unknown fields are rejected. Tokens are compared as sha256 digests with `secrets.compare_digest` against every key, without returning early. A file that cannot be read or is malformed raises the new `AccessKeysError`, whose message never echoes a value (a bad value might be the token itself). `cuemem serve` and `cuemem proxy` read the keys after `.env` is loaded and before the memory system opens, and a broken file prints "错误：…" and exits 1; scoped keys alone count as authentication for non-loopback binds; the startup line names the admin token and the keys. The file is read once at startup.
     - **How access is judged:** by the (user_id, agent_id) decoded from the space a request names (the new `sdk.space_owner`, the inverse of `space_for`, verified by a round trip); paths are checked after Starlette decodes them, so %2F, %252F and `..` are judged by the space they actually name. 'agent/x' always counts as agent x's own memory; otherwise a key for user x could read agent x's memory through `agent_id="agent"`, so scoped keys cannot use an agent literally named agent (that needs the admin token). Spaces that `space_for` cannot produce (evaluation spaces, names with two or more '/', non-canonical % escapes) are admin-only; a `{user: "*"}` scope covers plain-named spaces such as shop-demo.
     - **Every entry point:** a request that names a user, agent or space outside the scope gets 403: every `/api/spaces/{space}/…` route, `POST /api/sessions` (the default space included), `/api/spaces/import` (the ?space parameter or the file's space_id, before anything is written), and every `/api/v1` route that takes user_id or agent_id (including those inside filters, resolved with the SDK's own `_scope`, so the space checked is the space the SDK reads). Memory ids and session ids that live outside the scope behave exactly like missing ones: 404, history returns [], and the session message and end routes check before uploads are saved. `/api/spaces` and `/api/v1/spaces` are filtered, `/api/v1/reset` is admin-only, and import checks the target the way `Memory.load` picks it. The proxy: the user field, `X-CueMem-User`, `X-CueMem-Agent` and the configured default user must be in scope, otherwise an OpenAI-format 403 (type permission_error, code forbidden, param user) comes before recall, session opening and forwarding; a non-string user also gets 403 for scoped keys; requests without a user still pass through without touching memory; `/v1/models` and `/v1/embeddings` work for any valid key. The UI: a scoped key signs in with its name as the username, while the admin token accepts any username (signing in under a key's name gives that key's view); every event handler (start, end, send, load memory, forget and the forget choices) checks the space, and the chat session's own space is checked again (the review added a test), direct `/gradio_api` calls included; on page load the space box switches to the key's first concrete space when the default space is outside the scope. `principal_of` fails closed (500) on a route mounted without the guard instead of treating the caller as admin.
     - **No shared vector collections** (review): Qdrant collection names used to be a 40-character prefix plus a 32-bit hash, so two spaces could share a collection, and a scoped key that can choose agent_id could reach another tenant's collection with about 2^32 offline SHA-1s, then delete its vectors or pollute its top-k (by construction, a birthday pair is found in milliseconds). New collections are named `space_<prefix>_<128-bit stable_id>`. Collections with the legacy name (`VectorStore.legacy_name`, the evaluation builds under data/ included) keep working, but only for a space that proves ownership read-only: a few of its database units (rounds first, `MemoryDB.some_unit_ids`) exist as points in that collection; nothing is written or migrated, a space whose new-name collection exists always uses it, and the result is cached per space and dropped by delete_space. `MemorySystem.delete_space` and `cuemem demo --fresh` now delete the vectors before the database rows. Checked on a scratch copy of a LoCoMo evaluation build: conv-42 still resolves to its original `space_locomo_conv-42_3bc01e89` with 996 points; the copy was deleted afterwards. Isolation between tenants no longer rests on a 32-bit hash.
     - **Changed defaults:** with only `CUEMEM_API_TOKEN`, or no tokens at all, behaviour is unchanged. New public names: `cuemem.api.auth` (Keys, Principal, Scope, ADMIN, guard, principal_of, ui_login, TOKEN_ENV, KEYS_FILE_ENV, KEYS_ENV), `sdk.space_owner`, the `keys=` parameter of `create_app` and `create_proxy_app` (`token=` still works), the optional principal of `MemoryProxy.complete / stream / respond` and `build_ui(access=)`; the private helpers `app._token_check`, `app._ui_login`, `app._same` and `app.TOKEN_ENV` are gone, and the constant now lives in `cuemem.api.auth.TOKEN_ENV`.
     - **Unchanged:** MCP stays stdio, one space per process (`cuemem mcp --space`); it does not go through HTTP, and scoped keys (`CUEMEM_API_KEYS_FILE`, `CUEMEM_API_KEYS`) do not apply to it.
     - **Residual risk:** Gradio serves media files at `/gradio_api/file=<path>` to any signed-in user. Paths are content hashes (sha256) under the media directory, so a scoped user can fetch another tenant's image only by already knowing its hash.
     - **Evaluation:** evaluations never go through HTTP; requests and cache keys are unchanged.
  7. **Proxy coverage** (R13; revises D-062):
     - **Unknown models:** every `/v1` response carries `X-CueMem-Model` with the upstream model id actually used (non-streaming, streaming, pass-through without a user, Responses and embeddings). Unknown models are still remapped to the agent role's model by default, and the `cuemem.api.proxy` logger warns once per model name (at most 1,000 names are remembered). A new setting, `proxy.unknown_model: remap | reject` (default remap, documented in configs/default.yaml; the environment variable `CUEMEM_PROXY__UNKNOWN_MODEL`; the new `--unknown-model` flag of `cuemem proxy` and `cuemem serve`, which takes precedence): with reject, the proxy returns 400 with OpenAI's code `model_not_found`. The model is checked before recall, so a rejected request opens no session, recalls nothing and writes nothing; a request without a model still uses the default model.
     - **/v1/embeddings:** goes to the configured embedder (document vectors) and answers in OpenAI format; encoding_format float or base64 (the official SDK asks for base64 by default); `dimensions` must equal the model's native size; token arrays, empty strings and more than 2,048 inputs get 400; a rate limit returns 429 with Retry-After, and other embedder errors 502. It calls the embedder underneath CachedEmbedder, so callers' texts never enter the embedding cache, where forgetting could not purge them. `/v1/models` also lists the embedding model.
     - **A subset of /v1/responses:** non-streaming text input (a string, or message items with input_text / output_text parts; developer is mapped to system), plus instructions, max_output_tokens, temperature, top_p and user. The request is translated to Chat Completions and goes through the same recall, injection, forwarding and write-back, and the answer becomes a Responses object that validates against `openai.types.responses.Response` (finish_reason=length becomes status incomplete; cached and reasoning tokens are mapped). metadata, store, parallel_tool_calls, service_tier, prompt_cache_key and safety_identifier are accepted and ignored; everything else (stream, tools, previous_response_id, structured text formats, images, function_call items and so on) returns 501 with an OpenAI-format error naming the unsupported part and the header `x-should-retry: false`, so the official SDK does not retry the 5xx. Upstream errors pass through unchanged and write nothing.
     - **Changed defaults:** one more header on every `/v1` response; one more entry in `/v1/models`; model resolution in the proxy now runs before recall; new endpoints `POST /v1/embeddings` and `POST /v1/responses` (on both the standalone proxy and serve). Chat Completions requests sent upstream are byte-for-byte unchanged.
     - **Evaluation:** evaluations never use the proxy.
- **Alternatives:**
  - **An idempotency key for online writes** (each call carries a key, so a repeated call is recognised and not written again): every entry point would have to generate and store keys, and a failed call would still leave half-written turns. With embedding and the vector upsert first and one transaction after, a failed call leaves nothing, and the caller can simply retry.
  - **Leaving timeout retries to the SDK:** with a fallback model, the kind of input that derails generation (D-050) waits about 4 × timeout_s before switching; waiting only pays off for calls without a fallback model.
  - **Tagging forget's cache entries by session** (the example in this item's scope): an entity descriptor's name, type and description can come from different sessions (the type is upgraded from other, the description is filled in later), and the database records no entity provenance, so tags cannot be made complete; tags would also break silently if a future prompt added text to a request. "Keep only what the replay reuses byte for byte" is a stronger argument and needs no tags.
  - **A derived-memory version stored in the database** (the audit's proposal, a meta key): evaluation space files would gain rows. An in-process counter plus `PRAGMA data_version` and a rollback counter leaves the database contents and schema unchanged and still detects writes by other processes.
  - **Reusing the live in-run state as the mirror:** a cold load orders facts by (recorded_at, id) and re-embeds changed entity descriptors, so reusing the in-run state would change requests; the mirror is rebuilt in cold-load order (database id order).
  - **403 for out-of-scope ids:** fact ids are sha1(space, 'fact', session, statement), and with a caller-chosen run_id they can be computed, so a 403-versus-404 difference would tell a caller whether another tenant holds a given memory; only requests that name a space or user get 403.
  - **Expiry by rewriting valid_to during consolidation:** that would change derived memory and its requests; expired memories are excluded when the SDK reads instead.
  - **Only a 501 stub for /v1/responses:** the scope allowed a subset if a correct, tested one fit; everything outside it still gets 501 (not 400).
  - **The audit's default / error / passthrough for unknown models:** passthrough would send a name that is not in the model catalogue to some upstream, against forwarding by the catalogue; only remap and reject.
  - **A JSON family for README-only claims:** their wins and losses exist only in README text, so they were recomputed and stated in the docs.
- **Consequences:**
  - Code and tests: 93 files changed (not counting docs), 120 new test functions; 849 offline tests (parametrised cases included), of which 831 pass and 18 are skipped because the external baselines' optional packages are not installed; ruff and mypy pass. Every behaviour fix was checked to fail on the code before it.
  - **Evaluation requests and published numbers are unchanged:** after the merge of the two lanes and the review fixes, `FROZEN_REQUESTS` in `tests/test_eval_leak.py`, `tests/test_frozen_config.py` and the golden cache-key values in `tests/test_llm_client.py` and `tests/test_embedding.py` all pass, and none was re-pinned; the N2–N12 numbers still replay from data/cache. Consolidation also has the read-only replays of the 414 spaces and the 66 multi-run replays above, and retrieval the bitwise comparison of 9,006 results.
  - **Requests that change on purpose** are only on the failure path, on a cache miss: a call with a fallback model switches after one timeout of the primary. Requests the proxy sends upstream are unchanged; in reject mode an unknown model is no longer forwarded.
  - One accidental live request during the proxy work: an early draft of the CLI test posted to the proxy app in remap mode, which forwarded to the configured upstream with the test fixture's placeholder credentials. The committed test sends HTTP only to reject-mode apps and makes no network calls.
  - **Left for later:**
    1. The nominal embedding cost depends on the embedding cache (item 10 of D-068, against D-010): counting hits at nominal cost needs token counts, which the embedding cache never stored, and recomputing them means paid re-embedding; the effect is about $0.008;
    2. Development/test twins (item 11): the test sets are already spent, so only an offline sensitivity analysis is possible; future splits group by base question id (without `_abs`) and deduplicate generated hard negatives against the development pool;
    3. Most developer-facing text is Chinese (item 16): the README says the CLI's output is in Chinese on purpose; instead of translating the text in the code, there is now an English API reference ([api.md](api.md); Chinese edition [../api.md](../api.md));
    4. A TypeScript client (the other half of item 12); MCP over HTTP behind scoped keys, with several spaces per process (the other half of item 14);
    5. Skipped or partial in the implementation:
       - databases half-written by the old code (turns without rounds or jobs) are not repaired automatically; deleting orphan vectors is best-effort and assumes the caller serialises writes to one session (the SDK's session lock), which is not safe with several processes writing one database (the existing premise); `ConcurrentWriteError` is still raised after the commit and only matters for cross-process collisions;
       - calls without a fallback model (the agent's reply on HTTP chat, the reader, the judge) still retry timeouts max_retries times, so an agent turn whose model hangs can take about 4 × timeout_s before the API returns 504; there is no fallback on connection or 5xx errors once retries are exhausted; the three integrations' own memory tools do not get the provider-error messages; connection failures map to 502, not 503;
       - when forgetting fails after deleting turns but before the replay (for example reindex_sessions during an embedding outage), traces and the embedding cache are not cleaned, and a retry finds no targets (the LLM cache is now settled in that case);
       - the caption-route skip looks at database units, not Qdrant: a space whose caption vectors were orphaned (vector written, row rolled back) and that has no image units at all now reports p_caption_top1 = 0; the probe-to-L2 reuse does not see a write that lands during the planner call (sub-queries still do; L1 already packs the probe's candidates this way);
       - expiry applies only to SDK search, get_all and the profile; edit_of resolves only in the same space name and with the same extraction;
       - a run opened remotely and never ended stays open until the server restarts and adopt_open picks it up (no idle timeout); `/api/v1/reset` is admin-only rather than resetting a key's own spaces; the keys file is not hot-reloaded and there is no command to mint keys (use `printf %s "$TOKEN" | sha256sum`); local `Memory.search` accepts an invalid mode such as 'l9' and silently runs L2, while HTTP rejects it with 422;
       - dataset files already on disk are not re-hashed on download or on load, and there is no override flag;
       - README-only claims (N5, N6 and N9's pooled controller comparison) are in no JSON family; a cluster bootstrap and repeated builds were not done; the N11 latency table was not re-run.
  - **Remaining limits:**
    - local Qdrant renormalises the whole collection in place on every search (group 3), so the order of exactly tied results depends on how many searches the process has made;
    - failed write-backs of the proxy and `CueMemSession` live only in process memory and are lost when the process exits;
    - the Consolidator's mirror and the digest memo assume that code writing facts, entities or entity units, or changing turns, goes through the counted MemoryDB methods or bumps the counters itself (`_bump`, `_turn_writes`, `_turn_epochs`, as both docstrings say); otherwise the mirror or the memo can be stale;
    - adopting sessions left open by a crash, allocating turn numbers under a per-session lock and deleting orphan vectors all assume one process writes a database at a time (as in D-068);
    - LongMemEval space 853b0a1d cannot be replayed read-only: before and after the change it stops at the same extraction request that is not in data/cache; under the qwen preset, the replay of 7527f7e2 already differs from the published database before the change. Neither is related to this round.
- **After the third audit round (D-070):**
  - **Group 1:** "the same call can be retried without duplicating turns" was not true when the trace write or the next turn number failed after the commit; both are fixed. "28 scripted failure sequences" were not in the test suite (3 were); the rules are now pinned by `tests/test_llm_client.py::test_retry_rules_follow_the_sdk`.
  - **Group 3 (R5):** a forget could run between the probe and the pack of a raised search; both retrievals now run under one read hold.
  - **Group 4:** the pooled controller comparison is now a family in `n9-final/results-holm.json` ("三个记忆数据集合并：控制器", +60 / −37 p = 0.025, +15 / −64 p < 0.001); the MemLens 128K raw p is 0.022, not 0.023; the other text-only N9 comparisons (+33 / −6, +47 / −10, +168 / −36, +9 / −11, +56 / −44) cannot change verdict under any correction.
  - **Left for later:** item 4 is done in reduced form (a typed OpenAPI snapshot from which TypeScript types are generated; no hand-written npm package) and MCP over HTTP behind scoped keys at `/mcp`; in item 5, the forget gap after deleting turns is closed (`reindex_sessions` is gone), remote runs end after 30 idle minutes, and `/api/v1/reset` resets a scoped key's own spaces; the keys file is still read only at startup, `search(mode="l9")` still runs L2 locally, and README-only claims remain only for N5 and N6.
  - **Consequences:** the 18 tests skipped for the external baselines' packages now run in CI's "外部基线" workflow; "the N2–N12 numbers still replay from data/cache" should read: request bytes and cache keys are pinned, the N12 spaces were replayed read-only, and the N2–N11 replay was not re-run for lack of the original cache.
  - **Remaining limits:** of the two non-replayable spaces, 853b0a1d under the default preset now replays read-only (87 lookups, 1 deepseek-flash fallback hit, 214 facts equal); 853b0a1d under the qwen preset was refused by both models, and refusals are not cached; 7527f7e2 under the qwen preset is shadowed by a later primary reply in the cache (fixing it would need per-protocol cache keys).
- **After the fourth audit round (D-071):**
  - **Group 1:** "messages from before a forget are never written" has one exception for the proxy: the user's message of a turn whose forget raced the upstream call is written, and only the answer is dropped.
  - **Group 3 (R7):** "prints how to install the UI (`uv sync --extra ui` or `pip install "cuemem[ui]"`)": outside a checkout the hint is now `pip install "cuemem[ui] @ git+https://github.com/FlickYan/cuemem"`, because CueMem is not on PyPI and the bare name would resolve to whoever registers it; inside the Docker image it is a rebuild with `--build-arg EXTRAS=…`.
  - **Group 6:** a scoped key is not a spending limit: it limits whose memory a caller reaches, not what it spends (see D-071 for the optional per-key limits `rate_per_minute`, `models` and `max_output_tokens`).
  - **Left for later, item 5:** "a run opened remotely and never ended stays open until the server restarts and adopt_open picks it up" was not accurate: a restart alone adopted nothing. Since D-070 the run ends after 30 idle minutes; since D-071 a run that is open when the server stops survives the restart (the next add with its run_id continues it), and `cuemem serve` hands it to extraction one idle period after start-up if nobody does.

## D-070 The third audit round: a fresh seven-lens audit, 22 verified findings fixed; evaluation requests and published numbers unchanged

- **Status:** adopted (after N12).
- **Context:**
  - D-068 and D-069 audited the project and then worked through what the first audit had left. Neither looked again at the code they had added, nor at the parts of the product that grew around them (the SDK runs, `/api/v1`, scoped keys, the background worker, the proxy's retries). The third round started from scratch with the same seven lenses (core correctness; robustness under concurrency, resources and failures; security and privacy; performance; evaluation hygiene and reproducibility; product gaps; test quality) and the same rules: read the code only, reproduce every candidate offline (FakeAPI, ScriptedModel, read-only copies of databases and caches), measure how often it happens, and say whether the fix would change an evaluation request.
  - Every candidate went through adversarial verification: another reviewer re-ran the reproduction, looked for counter-examples, narrowed the claim and rewrote the fix where needed (the "refined change"), and judged whether it would break the invariant below. 22 findings were kept (T1–T22, 12 rated high and 10 medium); one sub-finding (relabelling H3, F46) was refuted and dropped.
  - The fixes were implemented in two lanes and a final step, then integrated: 8 of the 13 cherry-picks conflicted, and one merge commit added `status` to the typed async classes and the OpenAPI document and made a scoped reset forget only the idle-closed runs in its own spaces. A review of the merged result through three lenses (core, HTTP surfaces, evaluation) found 11 more problems: 9 changed code, each with a test that fails before its fix, and 2 were wrong numbers in the implementers' notes, corrected in these docs. The groups below describe the state after that review.
  - The invariant is D-068's: LLM and embedding requests under the default configuration and in evaluations stay byte-for-byte the same, and so do cache keys; the frozen methods (B1, B2, B4-nobm25 = L1, B5 = L2, B6, and the three controller files) keep their behaviour; every behaviour fix has a regression test checked to fail on the code before it; every deliberate change of a default is listed in its group. No paid API was called and no evaluation was run.
- **Decision:**
  1. **Reads, forgets and locks** (T13, T1, T2, T4; revises D-021, D-040, D-045, D-068 group 2):
     - **One lock order** (T13): a space's consolidation lock first, then its read gate. `forget` and `delete_space` take both in that order (`MemorySystem._exclusive`). While a forget waits for a background consolidation it does not hold the gate yet, so reads in that space keep going (a search in the timing reproduction went from about 2.9 s to 3 ms); once it holds the consolidation lock it waits for reads in flight and new reads queue behind it, so D-068's guarantee still holds. Calling `forget`, `delete_space` or `consolidate` (and so SDK `add(wait=True)`, `update`, `end_run(wait=True)`, `close_session(wait=True)`) from a thread that holds `reading()` of the same space raises RuntimeError instead of deadlocking.
     - **A raised search reads under one hold** (T1): when SDK `search` or `recall_listing` (MCP recall, the LangGraph and OpenAI Agents recall, `/api/v1/memories/search`, `MemoryClient`) raises L0 to L1, both retrievals and the reads of metadata and the profile run under one read hold. Before, a forget could run between the probe and the pack (an R5 regression from D-069) and the search returned facts and excerpts it had just deleted, and wrote a retrieve trace after the forget's cleanup. `MemorySystem.retrieve(candidates=...)` packs the candidates only when the caller holds `reading(space)`; otherwise it searches again (the pre-R5 path, same result).
     - **Replays re-register the jobs they had** (T2): `Consolidator.rebuild` deletes the derived vectors first, then reads the jobs and clears derived memory in one transaction, and re-registers jobs at once through `Consolidator.requeue`, shared with forget. Only sessions that had a job are enqueued, and pending, failed and content-filter-skipped outcomes are restored as they were. Before, one vector-store error in the replay of a grown session wiped every fact, entity and profile line of the space until an explicit consolidate; forget's replay extracted `infer=False` sessions and re-sent sessions that moderation had refused (two extra requests to two providers per delete).
     - **Forget in three phases** (T4): first the network steps, with the database untouched (`OnlineWriter.prepare_rounds` embeds and upserts the rounds that lose a turn, in one call with the same texts and order as before; then the vectors of replaced rounds, of all derived units and of the doomed images are deleted); then the LLM cache (delete the affected scopes, hold the reconciliation and entity entries, D-069); then, under `_open_lock`, one transaction swaps the rounds, deletes the turns, clears derived memory and re-registers the jobs. After the commit come image-file deletion (a file that cannot be deleted is logged on the `cuemem.memory` logger and noted as `images_left` in the trace), the remaining vector deletes and the replay. Unfinished replay work (a replay that raised, or sessions whose extraction failed) is handed to the background worker when one runs. Before, a forget that failed after deleting the turns left facts derived from them current, searchable and impossible to delete by id, the SDK reported success, and nothing ever repaired it.
     - **A failed transaction keeps the reconciliation cache** (review): the new `ResponseCache.release` drops this forget's hold markers and lock file without deleting entries, and forget calls `settle` only after a commit. Before, a failed transaction still deleted every held reconciliation and entity entry of the space although the log and derived memory were unchanged. What such a failure does lose: the extraction cache of the affected sessions, the space's answer and forget caches and the derived vectors were deleted before the transaction; the retry's replay rebuilds them, and until then search does not find those derived memories.
     - **Changed defaults:** a forget that fails before its commit (embedding outage, vector-store or database error) raises and deletes nothing, even with `report_rebuild_errors=True`, so it can be retried as is; forget's replay no longer extracts sessions that never had a job (SDK `infer=False`) and no longer re-sends sessions that moderation skipped, and failed jobs keep their attempts and backoff (explicit `consolidate()`, `add(wait=True)` and `update()` still extract the whole space); a vector-store error during a replay leaves the space as it was and the worker retries it; SDK `delete`, `DELETE /api/v1/memories/{id}` and `MemoryClient.delete` return `"Nothing deleted"` with a warning to run `consolidate(rebuild=True)` when no turn was deleted but the fact still exists; the forget, delete-space and consolidate calls inside `reading()` raise RuntimeError. New internals: `MemoryDB.derived_unit_ids`, `Consolidator.requeue`, `OnlineWriter.prepare_rounds` (`reindex_sessions` is gone), `ResponseCache.release`.
     - **Evaluation:** evaluations never forget, replay or hold a read gate across calls. For the four forget shapes (user turn, assistant turn, whole session, by fact), the inner embedding calls, the chat request hashes and the end state (turns, units, facts, vectors, jobs) are byte-identical before and after T4.
  2. **Durability, residue and status** (T5, T6, T10, T14; revises D-012, D-021, D-045, D-046, D-061, D-068 groups 2 and 3, D-069 groups 1 and 5):
     - **Nothing raises after a commit** (T5): a trace that cannot be written (disk full, no permission, a bad traces path) logs one warning on the `cuemem.core.trace` logger (once per run of failures) instead of failing a committed write or a completed forget, or hiding the original exception. SDK `add` and `update` write the next turn number inside the same transaction as the turns, so a failure there rolls the whole write back. D-069's "the same call can be retried without duplicating turns" was not true when either of these failed after the commit; now it is, and `ConcurrentWriteError` remains the only post-commit raise.
     - **delete_all and reset leave nothing that names the user** (T6): `delete_space` (behind `delete_all`, `reset`, `DELETE /api/v1/memories` and `POST /api/v1/reset`) also deletes, in the same transaction, the space's turn-number rows, its `consolidation:<space>` fingerprint and its write counters, then the open-session marks left by a crash (marks of runs still open in this process are kept), then every trace line whose `attrs.space` is the space together with the query vectors of its question (`MemorySystem.purge_space_traces`; `reset` makes one pass over all spaces). A forget that removes all of a session's turns also deletes that session's turn-number row. The SDK writes turn numbers under `next-idx:{len(space)}:{space}:{session}` and still reads the legacy `next_idx:` rows, which removes a cross-tenant collision (user `acme` with run `alice:x` and user `acme:alice` with run `x` shared one row, so one user's writes made the other's turn numbers skip). MemoryDB keeps at most 100,000 per-session write counters (`TURN_WRITES_KEPT`). Review: a reset that fails partway still purges the traces of every space it had started deleting.
     - **Background failures are visible** (T10): every failed background extraction is logged once per (space, error type) as an English WARNING on `cuemem.write.consolidate`, ending "see Memory.status()", and logged again only after a consolidation of that space extracts at least one session with none failing. New `Memory.status(*, user_id, agent_id)` (also on `AsyncMemory`, `MemoryClient`, `AsyncMemoryClient` and `GET /api/v1/status`): `pending`, `failed` (per session: `session_id`, `attempts`, the first line of `error`, and `retry_at`, the time of the automatic retry, or null when attempts reached 3, the pipeline version differs or no worker runs), `background` and the last 3 `errors` of this space. `add(wait=True)` whose own session failed extraction returns `status: "queued"` with `error` (it used to return `consolidated`); `update()` returns `error` too. Logs, `worker.errors` and (review) the `consolidate_failed` trace event keep only the first line of an error: later lines of a structured-output error quote model output, and so the user's words, which forgetting did not remove from the trace. Security: `/api/spaces/{space}/status` used to show the last 3 errors of all spaces (other tenants' space ids and quoted model output); it now shows only that space's, and forget and delete_space drop them.
     - **Idle sessions end under `cuemem serve`** (T14): UI and `/api` chat sessions, `/api/v1` runs and the sessions of the `/v1` proxy mounted in serve end after 30 idle minutes and go to extraction (a daemon thread, `cuemem-api-sweep`, checks every minute). A later message to an ended `/api` session gets 404 saying that idle sessions end; the UI starts a new session and says why; the next `add` with the same `run_id` reopens an idle-closed `/api/v1` run, which is extracted again in full when it ends. New flag `cuemem serve --idle-minutes` (default 30; must be > 0, otherwise exit 1 before the memory system opens); `create_app(idle_minutes=None)` disables the sweep. `Memory.close_idle_runs(idle_s)` is local only, and the local SDK never calls it itself.
     - **Changed defaults:** trace-write failures are warnings; delete_all and reset also delete the space's traces, turn-number rows, fingerprint and crash-left marks; the new turn-number key format; background failures log a warning; `add(wait=True)` reports a failed extraction of its own session as `queued` with `error`; `/api/spaces/{space}/status` is per space; under `cuemem serve`, sessions and runs idle for 30 minutes end (before, a remote run never ended until a restart, and the mounted proxy ended an idle session only on its next request).
     - **Evaluation:** evaluations use `tracer=None`, never call `delete_space`, never construct the SDK's `Memory` and never go through `create_app`; requests and cache keys are unchanged.
  3. **Scale and long-running servers** (T19, T20, T18, T8; revises D-005, D-020, D-046, D-056, D-069 group 2):
     - **Per-request work no longer grows with the sessions in a space** (T19): MemoryDB gains `has_sessions`, `session_started`, `sessions_of`, `turns_versions`, `has_jobs`, `has_jobs_of_other_version` and `enqueue_jobs`; SDK `add` and `search`, the proxy, the three integrations' emptiness checks, `Consolidator.enqueue`, `stale()` and `_run` use them. Measured at 20,000 sessions: `add` 58 → 6 ms, `add(wait=True)` 872 → 473 ms, `stale()` 100 → 3 ms, enqueueing one session 22 → 0.05 ms, a consolidation with nothing pending 499 → 160 ms, and the longest SQLite lock hold 232 → 35 ms. Review: re-registering jobs after clearing derived memory ran one supersede query per job over all pending jobs, so it grew with the square of the sessions (2,000 / 5,000 / about 20,000 jobs: 217 / 1,236 / about 20,000 ms); a new index on jobs (space_id, session_id), added when an existing database is opened (no schema-version bump), makes it 9 / 24 / 96 ms, and a forget at 20,000 sessions went from 28 s to 3.5 s, during which every other user's database access waited. The prefix scans, the status listing, export and the delete-all forget still read every session.
     - **Several spaces consolidate at once** (T20): the background worker runs `consolidation.background_workers` threads (default 4; `CUEMEM_CONSOLIDATION__BACKGROUND_WORKERS`) over a deduplicated queue: one space still runs one at a time in session order, repeated submits of a space are merged, a submit while it runs triggers exactly one more run, and a submit after `stop()` is ignored (before, `wait()` then hung). Provider concurrency is still capped per provider, and peak memory is up to 4 loaded run states. Measured: 20 users at once, median 5.9 → 1.6 s and last 10.6 → 2.7 s; 30 background adds → 6 runs instead of 30; another user's extraction during a 600-session replay waits 0.02 s instead of 6–11 s. `MemorySystem.from_settings` now shares the text embedder's cache with the image embedder, so image cache writes no longer drop D-069's consolidation mirror (9,000 facts: 495 → 180 ms per consolidation after another user's photo recall). `background_workers` is excluded from the consolidation fingerprint (pinned at `2f0adc7890b6a52e`) and from the evaluation resume check.
     - **Traces** (T18): `TraceSink.remove` scans each daily file without the lock and rewrites, under the lock, only the files with removals (the old counter was cumulative, so every file after the first match was rewritten); it splits lines only on `\n` (the old `splitlines()` also split on U+2028, U+2029 and U+0085, which the trace writes unescaped, so forget missed traces and query vectors whose question contained one, and rewrites corrupted other spaces' lines); forget prefilters lines on the space id. Measured with 60,000 lines from 1,000 users in 31 files (58 MB): forget's trace pass 488–642 → 101–125 ms, a concurrent trace write's wait 488–641 → 7–10 ms, files rewritten 31 → 1. New opt-in retention, `traces_retention_days` (`CUEMEM_TRACES_RETENTION_DAYS`, an integer ≥ 1, unset by default = keep forever): `cuemem serve` and `cuemem proxy` delete daily trace files older than N days at start-up and then once a day, together with the query vectors of their questions; the LLM cache is untouched; SDK-only apps can call `memory.system.prune_traces(days)`. The serve and proxy sweep threads now run once at start-up, and the standalone proxy's survives errors.
     - **Open files in local mode** (T8): local-mode Qdrant keeps one open SQLite file per collection (two for spaces with photos), opens all of them at start-up and loads every vector into RAM (about 20 KB per unit at 1,024 dimensions). With the usual soft limits (1,024 on Linux, 256 on macOS) a multi-user `cuemem serve` stopped accepting users at about 1,000 or 250 spaces and then could not restart, nor could `migrate-vectors` open the store to move it. Every `cuemem` command now raises its soft open-file limit to the hard limit (at most 65,536; on macOS falling back to 10,240 and 4,096; never lowered; the library and SDK do not). A store that cannot open or create a collection raises the new `VectorStoreOpenError`, naming the collection count, the limits and the remedy (raise `ulimit -n`, or `cuemem migrate-vectors --to URL` and `CUEMEM_VECTOR_STORE__URL`), and releases the half-opened files (1,024 descriptors held before, 7 after). `cuemem doctor` prints the collection count against the limit, in yellow above half of it; `cuemem mcp` start-up errors go to stderr with exit 1. The remaining costs are qdrant-client's: `meta.json` is rewritten on every collection create, and RAM grows with all points; the real fix for many users is a Qdrant server.
     - **Changed defaults:** consolidation runs up to 4 spaces at once; the CLI raises its open-file soft limit; the serve and proxy sweep threads run at start-up; trace retention is off unless set. New `consolidation.background_workers`, `traces_retention_days`, `cuemem.errors.VectorStoreOpenError`, `MemorySystem.prune_traces` and `expire_traces`, `TraceSink.remove(..., contains=)` and `TraceSink.prune`, `build_image_embedder(cache=)`.
     - **Evaluation:** evaluations never start the worker. An equivalence run (a 2,000-session space plus 50 small users through add, a regrown replay, consolidate and forget) gave byte-identical jobs, facts, reports and 3,957 chat-request digests before and after T19; per-space facts, the jobs table and the 52 chat-request digests of a 12-user run are identical for the old single thread and `background_workers` 1, 2, 4 and 8.
  4. **UI and input-size security** (T3, T12; revises D-056, D-062, D-063, D-069 group 6):
     - **The UI checks file paths again** (T3): the UI is mounted with `gr.mount_gradio_app` and never launched, which left Gradio's own client-path check off. Any signed-in UI user, scoped tenants included, could have the server copy any readable file (`.env` with the admin token, the keys file) into Gradio's cache and download it, through a message's files or the Chatbot history: privilege escalation to admin. CueMem now sets `demo.has_launched = True` after mounting, and `tests/test_auth.py::test_ui_rejects_server_side_paths` pins the behaviour over HTTP, because the attribute is Gradio-internal.
     - **The token before the body** (T12): `/api` and `/api/v1` authenticate before FastAPI reads or parses the body (`auth.authenticated_route`), so a request without a valid token gets 401 even with a bad or huge body (bad JSON used to get 422). The OpenAPI document is unchanged.
     - **Body caps** (T12): every app built by `create_app` or `create_proxy_app` caps request bodies before reading them (`api/limits.py`, BodyLimit): imports (`/api/spaces/import`, `/api/v1/import`) at `CUEMEM_MAX_IMPORT_BYTES` (default 1 GiB), `/api/`, `/v1/` and `/gradio_api/upload` at `CUEMEM_MAX_BODY_BYTES` (default 64 MiB), every other path (Gradio's sign-in and event routes, `/mcp`) at a fixed 8 MiB. Above the cap: 413, with code `payload_too_large` under `/api/v1`. A bad value of either variable makes `cuemem serve` and `cuemem proxy` print "错误：…" and exit 1. Review: a parsed JSON body takes about 24 times its size in memory, so a scoped key could make the server hold about 25 GB with one 1 GiB import. Scoped keys now import at `CUEMEM_MAX_BODY_BYTES` (the 413 says that larger imports need the admin token), and the bodies one scoped key has in flight together may not exceed `CUEMEM_MAX_BODY_BYTES` (429 with `Retry-After: 1`, code `rate_limited` under `/api/v1`), so one key holds at most about 1.6 GB at the default. Admin and no-auth deployments are unchanged.
     - **Image size** (T12): images above Pillow's `Image.MAX_IMAGE_PIXELS` (89,478,485 by default, read at call time and never changed by CueMem) are refused with "Image too large": ValueError in the SDK and on import, 400 on `/api/v1`, 409 on `/api`. Nothing is stored, and a proxy or `CueMemSession` write-back carrying such an image is dropped alone (before, it blocked that session's later write-backs, which were all lost at close). Decodes above 16 million pixels run at most 2 at a time; output bytes are unchanged. Review: `/api` chat and the UI check every uploaded image (`cuemem.store.media.check_image`, header only) before recall, decoding or any model call; before, an image over twice the limit gave 500, and one between one and two times the limit was answered by the model and then refused.
     - **Changed defaults:** body caps with 413 on serve and the proxy; 401 instead of 422 for an unauthenticated bad body; scoped keys import at the body cap and have an in-flight budget (429); oversized images are refused; large decodes queue. New `create_app(max_body_bytes=, max_import_bytes=)`, `create_proxy_app(max_body_bytes=)`, `memories_router(route_class=)`, environment variables `CUEMEM_MAX_BODY_BYTES` and `CUEMEM_MAX_IMPORT_BYTES`.
     - **Evaluation:** evaluations never go through HTTP. Evaluation photo ingest goes through `MediaStore.put`, so the pixel cap applies to a rebuild: the 25 images in data/ and demo/ on the audit machine reach at most 589,824 pixels; the MemLens and N6 photo sets were not on it (a hit would fail loudly, not change a number). Persisted spaces and cache replays never call `put`.
  5. **Mem0 compatibility and the remote contract** (T11, T17; revises D-061, D-063, D-066, D-069 group 5):
     - **Config and arguments** (T11): an unknown key in a config mapping given to `Memory()`, `Memory.from_config()` or `AsyncMemory()` raises ValueError listing the dotted keys with suggestions, and a Mem0-format config (llm, embedder, vector_store provider/config, custom_instructions, …) gets a migration hint; before, it was silently ignored and the DeepSeek + Qwen defaults ran. `Settings` objects, `CUEMEM_*` variables and YAML stay lenient. `limit` and `top_k` must be positive integers (ValueError, HTTP 400; before, `limit=0` returned everything and `limit=-1` dropped one result). `search` and `get_all` take `show_expired=False`. `update(memory_id, text=None, *, metadata=None, data=None)`: `text` is Mem0 2.x's name and `data` an alias; a metadata-only update raises ValueError (was TypeError). `MemoryClient(base_url=None, *, api_key, host, timeout, http_client)`: `host` is an alias of `base_url`, and a URL without `http(s)://` fails at construction without echoing the value (`MemoryClient("m0-…")` would otherwise send a token as a URL).
     - **Every id that search returns works by id** (T17): `get` accepts excerpt and image ids (excerpt items have no score; all carry `space_id`), `history` is `[]` for them, `delete` forgets that round or image (`dry_run` too), and `update` of them raises ValueError. New `Memory.media(media_id) -> bytes | None`, `MemoryClient.media` and `GET /api/v1/media/{media_id}` (image bytes with their Content-Type; 404 outside the key's scope or for a non-image id): an image item's `path` is a server-local path. `MemoryAPIError` gains `retry_after` (the Retry-After header) and `type` (the server's exception class); a `base_url` ending in `/api/v1` is accepted; a 404 without an error type explains that `base_url` must be the root of `cuemem serve`, and a refused connection asks whether it runs. A malformed export raises `ValueError("Malformed export: …")`, `/api` 409 and `/api/v1` 400 (before, KeyError or TypeError, 404 or 500). The integrations raise TypeError at once when given a `MemoryClient` (before, an AttributeError later), and `claude.with_recall` raises instead of warning.
     - **Changed defaults:** unknown config keys raise; `limit`/`top_k` below 1 or bool raise; a metadata-only update raises ValueError; a non-http `MemoryClient` URL fails at construction; get and delete accept excerpt and image ids; a malformed import is ValueError; `MemoryAPIError` carries `retry_after` and `type`. `MemoryDB.fact_space` is replaced by `memory_kind`. There is still no `threshold` (scores only rank: L1 is rank fusion, about 0.016 at the top), Mem0's `rerank` is `mode="l2"`, and `update` takes no `expiration_date`.
     - **Evaluation:** evaluations never use the SDK or the client; requests and cache keys are unchanged.
  6. **MCP over HTTP and typed clients** (T21, T22; revises D-047, D-056, D-069 groups 5 and 6):
     - **MCP over HTTP** (T21): `cuemem serve` serves MCP over Streamable HTTP at `/mcp` (stateless, JSON responses), with the same four tools as `cuemem mcp` and byte-identical texts. It uses the `/api` token (401 otherwise); `X-CueMem-User` and `X-CueMem-Agent` choose the space as in the proxy, and a scoped key without them uses its own first user. The admin token, no-auth mode and wildcard-only keys must send `X-CueMem-User` (400 otherwise), an out-of-scope user or agent gets 403 and nothing is written, a cross-site Origin gets 403, and the Host is checked app-wide. `/mcp` falls in the fixed 8 MiB body tier, and the MCP SDK has its own 4 MiB limit. `cuemem serve --no-mcp` (or `create_app(mcp=False)`) turns it off; serve prints the URL at start-up. The stdio `cuemem mcp` is unchanged and still cannot share a local data directory with serve.
     - **Scoped reset** (T21): `POST /api/v1/reset` with a scoped key deletes the spaces in its scope and ends only the runs there (and forgets their idle-closed records), with message "All memories in this key's scope deleted" and the same keys; it used to return 403. A wildcard tenant key's reset now wipes the whole tenant instead of failing safe.
     - **Typed contracts** (T22): `/openapi.json` names a schema for every `/api/v1` 200 response (FactItem, ExcerptItem and ImageItem discriminated on `type`, SearchResult, AddResult, ForgetResult or DryRunResult, StatusResult, ExportDocument, …) and documents every error status (400, 401, 402, 403, 404, 409, 413, 422, 429, 502, 503, 504) as `V1ErrorBody`; FastAPI's HTTPValidationError, which never occurred, is gone; the media route is `image/*`. Runtime JSON is unchanged. A committed snapshot, `docs/openapi-v1.json` (20 operations, only `/api/v1` and its components), is regenerated by `scripts/openapi_v1.py`, whose `--check` the test suite enforces; TypeScript types come from `npx openapi-typescript docs/openapi-v1.json`. The wheel ships `py.typed` (classifier "Typing :: Typed"), so downstream type checkers now check CueMem calls and may report new, correct errors. `AsyncMemory` and `AsyncMemoryClient` declare every method as a typed coroutine with the same signature as the sync class (a test compares them both ways), with explicit constructors and the new `AsyncMemory.from_system`. Review: under `/api/v1`, a cross-site browser request (403, code `forbidden`) and a wrong Host header (400, code `invalid_request`, naming `CUEMEM_ALLOWED_HOSTS`) now have the `V1ErrorBody` shape too, so "every `/api/v1` error is V1ErrorBody" is true; `/api`, the proxy and the UI keep their replies.
     - **Changed defaults:** serve offers `/mcp` by default; a scoped key may reset its own spaces; OpenAPI is typed; the package is typed; the `/api/v1` host and origin refusals are JSON. New `cuemem serve --no-mcp`, `create_app(mcp=True)`, `cuemem.api.mcp.McpEndpoint`, `build_server(service, space_id=None)`, `docs/openapi-v1.json`.
     - **Evaluation:** no LLM or embedding request changes; evaluations use neither MCP nor HTTP.
  7. **Reproduction, claims and tests** (T9, T16, T7, T15; revises D-010, D-048, D-050, D-055, D-065, D-067, D-068 groups 1 and 6, D-069 groups 1 and 4):
     - **A read-only replay uses the fallback's cached reply** (T9): under `llm_cache_mode = read_only` (`--cache-only`), a primary cache miss also counts as a reason to fall back (`llm.client.fallback_errors`), in `LLMClient.structured`, the evaluation gateway and the Mem0 adapter; the fallback is recorded as `llm_fallback` with reason `CacheMissError`, and when both models miss, the CacheMissError names both (`replay_miss`). A primary that was refused or timed out at build time was never cached, so before, the cached fallback reply was never tried. LongMemEval space 853b0a1d under the default preset now replays read-only (87 lookups, 1 deepseek-flash fallback hit, 214 facts equal field by field). Other modes are unchanged.
     - **The reproduction script stops rewriting published files** (T9): `reproduce.Step` has `requires=` and skips a step whose runs have no `results.jsonl` (`--dry-run` marks it); `n11_variance.py`, and (review) `n11_mem0.py`, `n11_second_judge.py report` and `n12_leak.py` exit 1 and write nothing when a run or a dataset's verdicts are missing (before, they wrote `{}` or near-empty JSON over the published results on a machine without the runs); `n12_leak.py` treats the three runs that were never made as `NOT_RUN`. `n11_latency.py --out` and `n8_summary.py --out` / `--allow-cached`: the script writes the machine-dependent latency table and demo summary under `runs/`, and `n8_summary` refuses to overwrite the published `demo.json` from a replay with cache hits.
     - **Claims checked against the JSON** (T16, T7): new `routing.paired_diff_ci` (Newcombe's method 10); `n12_leak.py` writes `ci95` for every paired comparison and `pooled_cuemem` into `n12-leak/summary.json` (new keys only; `summary-holm.json` unchanged); N9's `results-holm.json` gains the family "三个记忆数据集合并：控制器" (+60 / −37, p = 0.025; +15 / −64, p < 0.001; only the whole-document family's m moves, 219 → 221); `second_judge-holm.json`'s primary-judge family says it only compares the judges and that primary-judge verdicts come from N9's families. The documents now say: the leak ablation's 95% intervals allow up to about 8 points of inflation for one method (L1 +2.0, [−3.7, +8.1]) and about 3–4 averaged over L1, L2 and the controller, with 5 abstention questions; the test sets had one frozen pass (MemLens was also reported in N6) and later post-hoc subsets; neither N12 protocol is fully neutral (deepseek-flash was CueMem's reader in N2–N9); "1/20 of the cost" is answer-time cost, and with the one-off write path (about $43 / $15 / $54 per 1,000 histories on LongMemEval / MemLens 32K / 128K) L1 costs 1.4× / 2.1× / 1.4× full context per question on those one-question-per-history benchmarks, while on LoCoMo it stays about 4× cheaper; the MemLens 128K controller-vs-L2 raw p is 0.022 (0.0225 rounded twice became 0.023 in D-069 and the report).
     - **Tests and CI** (T15): tables pin the documented contracts (the argument forwarding of every `MemoryClient` method over `/api/v1`; `{user: "*", agent: A}` against `{agent: A}` scopes; the filter operators at their boundaries; a 19-row `_retry_delay` table; 13-row `provider_failure` and `worth_retrying` tables; the proxy's end-of-session drop). All 35 mutants of the audit are now killed; before, 34 survived the full suite. D-069's "28 scripted failure sequences" were not in the suite (3 were); the rules are now pinned by `tests/test_llm_client.py::test_retry_rules_follow_the_sdk`. A network guard (`tests/netguard.py`) fails any offline test that reaches a non-loopback host or a proxy from the environment, even from a background thread; the offline session strips proxy variables and provider keys and sets `GRADIO_ANALYTICS_ENABLED=False` (8 UI tests used to send 16 analytics requests); `pytest -m live` is exempt; subprocesses inherit the stripped environment but are not guarded. A latent bug made the R6 mirror test pass only with real keys in the environment. New CI: the "外部基线" workflow runs the five external baselines' adapter tests offline against FakeAPI with pinned versions (weekly, on demand, and on PRs touching the evaluation code, the baseline tests or the lock file) and fails on any skipped test; Mem0's entry needs the spaCy model wheel and (review) downloads fastembed's `Qdrant/bm25` model in a `prepare` step outside the guard; a `qdrant-server` job in ci.yml runs `tests/test_qdrant_server.py` against `qdrant/qdrant:v1.19.1` (locally: set `CUEMEM_TEST_QDRANT_URL`).
     - **Changed defaults:** read-only replays fall back on a primary miss; the analysis scripts exit 1 on missing inputs; `reproduce.py` writes latency and the demo summary under `runs/`; offline tests refuse the network. New script flags `--out` and `--allow-cached`, the test-only variable `CUEMEM_TEST_QDRANT_URL`, `llm.client.fallback_errors` and `replay_miss`, `routing.paired_diff_ci`.
     - **Evaluation:** the fallback change applies only to `read_only`, which never sends a request; the generated JSON gained keys and one family, and no accuracy, win, loss or raw p changed.
- **Alternatives:**
  - **Per-thread read connections** (T19's phase 2): the real cross-user stall left after T19 is the VectorStore lock held across Qdrant queries, not SQLite; extra connections would add WAL readers without removing it.
  - **Per-key mirror invalidation and a fact-budget pool of mirrors** (T20): about 800 MB per 200k facts, and a shared cache connection already removed the invalidations that mattered.
  - **Expiring the LLM cache's `<space>|ask` rows with the traces** (T18): evaluation answer rows share that scope, and in a source checkout the same file (4,270 evaluation rows would have matched).
  - **A check in the UI's handlers** (T3): Gradio copies the file during preprocessing, before any handler runs, so it would block nothing.
  - **Tombstones for idle-ended sessions and Gradio's `demo.unload`** (T14): a 404 that says idle sessions end tells callers outside the scope nothing; `unload` cannot read `gr.State`.
  - **A hand-written TypeScript package** (T22): a typed OpenAPI snapshot plus generated types covers the contract; a package with retries and its own tests waits for demand.
  - **Committing per-question outcome files for every published run** (T9): the N2–N11 run directories are not on the audit machine; it needs its own decision.
  - **Per-protocol cache keys** so that 7527f7e2 replays under the qwen preset (T9): that changes cache keys.
  - **Adding the text-only N9 comparisons to the existing families, and re-running `n9_report.py`** (T16): it would shift adjusted p values cited in D-069 and the report, and without the N9 runs it would overwrite `results.json`.
